April 14, 2024 09:00 PM GMT

China Property Management & Services | Asia Pacific

# 2023 Results Recap – Silver Lining Emerging

2023 core earnings were better than expected at +14% y-y; earnings should bottom out with improving dividend payout ratios and share buybacks. Despite the macro challenges, we expect 13% earnings growth in 2024E with 6% dividend yield on average. Our preferred stocks are CR Mixc and PPS.

2023 core earnings were better than expected,+14% y-y on average, with SOE growth of 31%, while POEs dropped by 18%. Four key trends: 1) SOEs saw core margin expansion with better cost control; 2) several POEs still have special impairments with related developers; 3) improving OCF at 1.1x OCF/core profit on stronger cash collection and strategic focus on terminating businesses with weak cash flow; and 4) dividend payout ratios rose from 38% in 2022 to 47% in 2023.

Earnings to bottom out; impairments may linger for POEs but should not impact dividends for most companies: We believe the earnings reset is almost complete with an 11% gross profit contribution from DVAS (developer VAS) and non-recurring businesses, and our worst scenario earnings estimates indicating 10%/19% downside for POEs' 2024E earnings on different assumptions of CVAS (community VAS). Further impairments may linger for POEs on accounts receivable (AR) from related parties and goodwill, but given the impairments are non-cash, and most companies' dividends are based on core profit, we believe the impairment impact is limited.

SOEs hold the advantage in third-party expansion: Third-party expansion has softened amid rising competition and tightened budgets for corporates and governments, but SOEs are taking share. We think SOEs can maintain their expansion pace, with a +22% y-y rise in annualized new contracts in 2023, while POEs recorded a 28% decline due to their damaged brand in recent years.

buybacks: The OCF/core profit ratio improved to 1.1x in 2023 (vs. 0.8x in 2022), thanks to better fee collection without Covid's impact and more KPI-focus on cash flows. Property management companies (PMCs) target an OFC/core profit ratio of >1x going forward. With stronger cash flow, PMCs see room to improve dividend payouts from the current 47%, and some companies are buying back shares.

We expect 13% earnings growth in 2024E and 6% dividend yield; prefer CR Mixc and Property Poly Services: For 2024, we expect 12% revenue growth, driven by defensive PMS revenue growth of 13%, CVAS and DVAS growth of 3% and -8%. We forecast 13% earnings growth (SOEs: 16%, POEs: 0%) and a 10% earnings CAGR in 2024-26E (SOEs: 17%, POEs: 3%). After decoupling from related developers, we think PMCs' recurring business models and improving cash flow could bring re-rating opportunities. Sector is trading at 8x 2025E P/E; we prefer CR Mixc and PPS.

## Investment Thesis

## 2023 results better than expected; core profit increased by 14% y-y on average but with weakening top-line growth; impairment risks lingered for POEs

Property management companies (PMC) reported better-than-expected 2023 results, with core profit +14% y-y on average – SOEs still posted solid results of 31% growth, while POEs declined 18% y-y. We saw several key trends in the 2023 results:

1) Revenue growth slowed to 4% y-y from 27% in 2022 (+17% for SOEs and 2% for POEs) ( Exhibit 10 ). Segment wise, basic property management services (PMS) still recorded stable growth of +13% y-y; CVAS were -2% y-y with PMCs' strategic focus back to core business amid consumption headwinds; DVAS continued to be the main drag with a 15% decline on average (-5% for SOEs and -44% for POEs) ( Exhibit 11 , Exhibit 12 , Exhibit 13 ). The steady growth of PMS demonstrates its defensive business nature amid the macro downturn, while the growth of to B business (CVAS, DVAS and PFM, etc.) may be a swing factor and highly dependent on the overall macro environment.

2) Gross margin stabilized with a narrower decline after the sharp drop since 2022 ( Exhibit 14 ): gross margin fell -1.4ppts to 19.2% on average (SOE +0.6 ppt to 16.8%, POEs -0.8ppts to 19.8%). SOEs saw slight GPM expansion in 2023 with operating leverage and better cost control, while POEs' GPM declined on 1) lower revenue contribution from high-margin businesses (i.e. CVAS and DVAS), and 2) higher portion of vacancy property management fees, which dragged down overall gross margin. However, the decline narrowed from -6.5% in 2022 for POEs, due mainly to 1) stabilizing revenue mix with the focus back on core business, and less disruption from DVAS and non-recurring business, and 2) normalizing segment margins amid the earnings reset. Thus, we expect relatively stable margin trends ahead.

3) Related-party impairment lingered, but the impact was non-cash; most companies dividend payout ratios are based on core profit ( Exhibit 15 ): A-living, CGS, Jinke and Sunac Services still recorded related-party receivable/goodwill impairment in 2023. Greentown Service (GTS) also posted an Rmb240mn special impairment for historical investment but further impairment risk looks limited. Onewo recorded Rmb616 amortization for previous M&As. For PMCs with special impairment/amortization (A-Living, CGS, Onewo, Sunac Services), except A-Living, the others adjusted their dividend base to core profit (adding back special impairment and amortization). Thus, although the impairment risk may still linger for POEs, the impact should not affect dividends and cash flow.

4) Dividends raised: CR Mixc, Onewo, PPS and Ever Sunshine raised their 2023 dividend payout ratio given their strong cash flow. A-Living also resumed dividend payments with improving cash flow. However, POWC and Jinke missed their final dividends but we think they are likely to resume in 2024/2025.

5) Improving OCF: Average OCF/core profit ratio reached 1.1x in 2023, significantly improving from 0.8x in 2022 thanks to stronger cash collection and PMCs' strategic focus of terminating business with weak cash flow.

## POEs' earnings reset has almost completed with limited contribution from DVAS and non-recurring business; POEs' impairments may linger but they are non-cash, and dividends are normally based on core profit

We think further earnings downside for POEs is limited, given that non-recurring business accounted for only 11% of total gross profit in 2023 (vs. 16% in 2022), including 6% from DVAS, and 3% from non-recurring CVAS (property agency, carpark sales and home decoration). We expect the gross profit contribution from non-recurring business will account for 8% of gross profit in 2026E, including 3% from DVAS and 4% of property agency and home decoration in CVAS ( Exhibit 18 , Exhibit 19 , Exhibit 20 ).

For potential impairments from AR and goodwill, POEs have made 36% provisions for related party account receivables, and we think further impairment is likely given the difficulties of collecting fees from related developers.

• As of end 2023, AR from related parties stood at Rmb813mn on average, accounting for 24% for total AR, and 0.8x of 2024E core profit (POE average of 1.9x and SOE average of 0.3x) ( Exhibit 21 , Exhibit 22 ).

• The cumulative impairment ratio of total AR was 13% for POEs and 4% for SOEs, and the cumulative impairment ratio of related party AR was 36% for POEs: CGS, Jinker Services and Sunac Services have made more sufficient provisions for related party AR with 58-81% impairment ratios, indicating less impairment drag in the future ( Exhibit 23 ).

• Goodwill of Rmb1.6bn on average, accounting for 0.8x of 2024E core profit (POEs average of 2.6x and SOEs average of 0.5x) ( Exhibit 24 ).

However, as most companies with special impairments have changed dividend policy to pay dividends based on core earnings (adding back special impairments and amortization), further impairments should have no material impact on dividends and cash flow.

According to our worst-case scenario analysis, normalized worst-case net profit implies 6- 18% downside from 2023 core profit and 10-19% downside from 2024E core profit ( Exhibit 25 ). In our analysis, we assumed two scenarios:

## Scenario 1:

• PMS revenue: based on 2023 revenue;

• CVAS revenue: based on 2023 revenue, only includes recurring business – community living, community common areas, local catering, while excluding nonrecurring business – property agency, carpark space sales, decoration, and tourism;

• DVAS revenue: assuming zero revenue contribution;

• Other recurring business revenue: based on 2023 revenue, including commercial operational services, city services, education, software, three supplies and property management;

• Other non-recurring business revenue: assuming zero revenue contribution, e.g.,hardware tech; and

• Net margin: assuming normalized margins, using SOEs' margins as a benchmark.

## Scenario 2:

• Major assumptions are the same as in Scenario 1.

• Different assumption is for CVAS revenue – we assume it is 5% of residential property management revenue (vs. 16% in 2023), as PMCs are focusing back to core CVAS that can satisfy residents' basic needs and enhance their stickiness; thus, some diversified CVAS may be cut.

Based on worst-case scenario 1, POEs are trading at 5.1x P/E, and based on scenario 2, POEs are trading at 6.2x P/E.

Exhibit 25: Worst case scenario analysis – Scenario 1
![](images/9dbe37042d6ada1da8cb3efda3147dab919f2e377f845a1acaa7f961cd600e0d.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimate

Exhibit 26: Worst case scenario analysis – Scenario 2
![](images/1f6b2d6b84a7b60926b1520617c613348e9d13ac004e02fa1b61aefb2e3d6509.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimate

Non-residential and secondary markets to be the next markets to compete for thirdparty expansion; SOEs have a competitive advantage given their ample resources; ongoing contract terminations for POEs

With more focus on core business as PMCs decouple from property developers, thirdparty expansion has become the key growth driver for PMCs. Growth in third-party expansion softened in 2023 amid rising competition. The divergence between SOEs and POEs is widening ( Exhibit 27 ).

• SOEs are on track to achieve their expansion targets, with a +22% y-y increase in annualized new contracts, thanks to diversified project portfolios and resources from parent developers to penetrate public projects. CR Mixc, COPH and Poly Property Services' third-party new contract values were up 64%/33%/61% y-y respectively in 2023; Semi- SOE (GTS and Onewo) were flat/down y-y off a high base, but the overall value was still at an industry-leading level.

• POEs recorded 28% y-y declines in third-party expansion on average, due to intensifying competition and damaged brand names. A-Living, CGS and Sunac Services were down 48%/24%/63%, respectively, in 2023, while Jinke Services and Ever Sunshine's third-party expansion was more stable.

SOEs have a competitive advantage in third-party expansion given their stronger brand names and relationships with government as well as superior expertise and experience compared to POEs.

For POEs, we think the second stage of independent growth is yet to be proven, given: 1) The POE brand has been damaged due to the default of their related developers since 2021; and 2) a more competitive market, with a declining primary residential market size, excessive competition in secondary residential expansion, and SOEs' growing presence in non-residential expansion.

Moreover, POEs are terminating some low-quality, third-party projects for higher profitability and cash flow. Ever Sunshine, Jinke Services and Sunac Services terminated 14%/13%/8% of GFA under management in 2023, respectively. For POEs as a whole, GFA under management rose by 8% y-y (vs. SOEs +25% y-y) in 2023, on average ( Exhibit 28 ).

We forecast a 7% CAGR for GFA under management for POEs, and 16% for SOEs, for 2023-26E.

## We expect an earnings turnaround in 2024E, with 13% growth in 2024E and 6% dividend yield; 10% CAGR in 2023-26E and improving capital management

After almost two years of earnings decline, we expect an earnings inflection to become evident at the 2024 results, with average earnings growth of 13%/6%/8% in 2024E/25E/26E, respectively (Exhibit 27), or a 10% CAGR in 2023-26E (14% for SOEs and 5% for POEs).

• PMS will be the key growth driver, with 13% CAGR revenue growth, on a 9% CAGR for GFA under management in 2023-26E: 1) related developers contributed 10- 17mn sqm p.a. on average; and 2) third parties contributed c.35mn sqm p.a. on average. New GFA under management from related developers will contribute to 18% of new GFA in 2026E (vs. 31% in 2023), we estimate.

• Community VAS to see moderate growth of 5% CAGR along with GFA expansion.

• DVAS could still drag on revenue with a 7% CAGR decrease, but with a narrower decline of -15% in 2023.

With improving operating cash flow and a more cautious M&A strategy, the ratio of OCF/ core profit increased from 0.8x in 2022 to 1.1x in 2023. To enhance capital management efficiency, PMCs have actively adapted the following methods: 1) Share buybacks ( Exhibit 35 ): POEs cumulatively spent HK\$784mn on share buybacks in 2023 (or 0.9% of total shares on average), vs. HK\$307mn in 2022 (or 0.4% of total shares) – among them, Jinke Services did the highest amount at 4.6% of total shares, followed by GTS of 1.4% and CGS of 0.9%; and 2) raise dividends – we believe there is potential for CR Mixc and Sunac Services to raise their dividend payout ratios in 2024E.

Looking ahead, we expect PMCs to raise dividends further and conduct consecutive share buybacks to improve capital management and ROE.

# Changes to Key Stocks, Ratings and Price Targets

The industry is trading at 7.9x 2025E P/E, with a 10% earnings CAGR in 2023-26E (17% for SOEs and 3% for POEs); we believe current valuations reflect the moderating growth outlook and market concerns about the impact that China property developers will have on the market as a whole. We lower our PTs by -11%, rolled from 10x 2024E P/E to 9x 2025E P/E, on average. Our preferred names remain CR Mixc and PPS for their strong earnings growth and healthy cash flow.

We upgrade Greentown Services to OW from EW and raise our PT to HK\$4.38, on 12x 2025E P/E.

We downgrade Powerlong Commercial Management to EW from OW and lower our PT to HK\$2.72, on 3x 2025E P/E.

## Key Stocks

CR Mixc (1209.HK, PT HK\$39.82, 53% upside): We like CR Mixc for its: 1) comprehensive shopping mall exposure, with resilient retail sales performance and ramp up of its thirdparty expansion capability; 2) strong parent developer with SOE background, consolidating the market; and 3) high earnings CAGR in 2023-26E of 19% vs. the industry average of 9%, and its stable gross margin outlook.

Poly Property Services (6049.HK, PT HK\$44.07, 62% upside): We like Poly Property Services for its: 1) strong parent developer with an SOE background, consolidating the market; 2) unrivaled competitive edge and strong expansion track record in public projects and ramping-up of its secondary residential and office building expansion, which should partially offset any shortfall in primary residential space; 3) strong operating cashflow and healthy AR balance, which has alleviated market concerns about cash collection; and 4) steady growth of 10% CAGR in 2023-26E (vs. the industry average of 9%), and a relatively stable gross margin outlook with limited downside risk.

## Rating changes

We upgrade Greentown Services to OW from EW and raise our PT to HK\$4.38 from HK \$3.46 on 2025E P/E of 12x, with 8% earnings CAGR in 2023-26E . We raise our 2024E/2025E earnings by 17%/8%, respectively, to Rmb998mn/Rmb1,065mn, and we forecast Rmb1,203mn profit for 2026E, to reflect: 1) solid growth in PMS thanks to strong third-party expansion of c.Rmb4bn new contracts p.a., 2) margin expansion and lower SG&A ratio with improving operating leverage and better cost control. We upgrade from EW to OW to reflect 1) earnings turnaround with higher visibility, and 2) better execution from the new management team, demonstrated by their on-track margin expansion.

We downgrade Powerlong Commercial Management to EW from OW and lower our PT to HK\$2.72 from HK\$3.90 on 2025E P/E of 3x, with 3% earnings CAGR in 2024-26E . We cut our 2024E/2025E earnings by 11%/14%, respectively, to Rmb509mn/Rmb534mn, and we forecast Rmb560mn profit for 2026E, to reflect: 1) moderate commercial PMS growth with low-single -digit SSSG and c.10 new mall openings p.a., and 2) relatively stable gross margin on stable occupancy rate and rental. The company missed its final dividend in 2023 as parentco is under restructuring. Given the mediocre growth outlook, dividend yield is a key driver for the stock, in our view. We could turn more constructive once the company has a clearer dividend policy after the parentco restructuring is settled. Move to EW.

## PT and earnings estimate revisions

On average, we lower our 2024 earnings estimates by 11% and our 2025 estimates by 13% to reflect: 1) lower GFA contribution from related developers due to weak contract sales and muted land acquisitions amid a prolonged recovery in the property market, and fierce competition in third-party expansion as the size of the new residential projects market shrinks; 2) normalized growth for community VAS with a focus back on traditional recurring business with a relatively stable penetration rate; 3) further slowdown in DVAS; and 4) gross margin pressure with more non-residential and third-party projects and lower margin for CVAS and DVAS as a result of less support from related developers.

For our price target changes, we apply 13x 2025E P/E on average for SOE and semi-SOE companies to reflect: 1) a solid earnings CAGR of 17% in 2023-26E with high visibility; 2) steady support from parentcos amid consolidation among top developers; and 3) proven track record of third-party expansion and competitive edge in non-residential market leveraging parentco resources. For POEs, we apply a multiple of 5x 2025E P/E on average (similar to the previous level), to reflect: 1) slower earnings growth of 3% CAGR for 2023- 26E, with margin pressure; 2) remaining concerns around potential impairment of relatedparty AR, loans and goodwill from past M&A; and 3) ongoing contract termination of lowquality projects and a lack of competitive advantages in third-party expansion.

## HK\$1.95PRICE TARGET

HK\$1.95: Base case, applying a 2.5x target P/E to our 2025 EPS estimate. We arrive at our target P/E multiple based on our scorecard for China's property management companies, which is based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro <sup>fi</sup>le, parent developer, <sup>fi</sup>nancials and cash <sup>fl</sup>ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro <sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We expect a moderate 2023-26E earnings CAGR of 10% vs. sector average of 9%. ▪ Margin compression in CVAS and DVAS may continue in the following few years. ▪ DVAS segment may further shrink amid weak property sales.

## Risk Reward Themes

Contrarian: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 7.0x 2025E P/E

## HK\$5.47

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected growth in GFA under management, driven by faster-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increases as property owners value high-quality service more, which ultimately leads to faster-thanexpected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry from more IPOs and rising social and government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 2.5x 2025E P/E

## HK\$1.95

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by on-track third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## 2.0x 2025E P/E

## HK\$1.56

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected growth in GFA under management, hampered by a slower delivery schedule from Agile and Greenland, along with weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

## HK\$6.71PRICE TARGET

Our price target is based on an 11x P/E applied to our FY25 EPS estimate of HK\$0.56. Our target P/E multiple is derived from our industry scorecard, based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro <sup>fi</sup>le, parent developer, <sup>fi</sup>nancials and cash <sup>fl</sup>ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro<sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We expect steady growth at a 17% earnings CAGR over 2023-26E, backed by its demonstrated capabilities in third-party expansion.

▪ The company’s improving fundamentals are exhibited in accelerating third-party expansion.

▪ We see market preferences for SOE names with limited liquidity concerns and af liated developers amid ongoing property credit tightening.

![](images/35f3b1820e57da8ae64b53d89294bfa72f178f52a5417807e2105a30e6e85a53.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 15x 2025E P/E

HK\$9.15

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected GFA under management growth driven by fasterthan-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increase as property owners value more high service quality, which ultimately leads to fasterthan-expected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry due to more IPOs and rising social and government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 11x 2025E P/E

## HK\$6.71

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable GFA under management growth backed by steady support from its sister companies, COLI and COGO, and on-track third-party GFA expansion. We expect relative stable property management fees ahead with no signi cant fee increase in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$3.66

## 6x 2025E P/E

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected GFA under management growth dragged by slower delivery schedule from COLI/COGO and weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

# Risk Reward - China Resources Mixc Life<sub>Risk Reward – China Resources Mixc Lifestyle Services (1209.HK)</sub>

(1209.HK)Leading Shopping Mall Manager with a Luxury Advantage

## HK\$39.82PRICE TARGET

Based on 20x 2025E P/E. Our target multiple is derived from our industry scorecard, based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro le, parent developer, nancials and cash ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro <sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme. The higher a company’s score, the higher the target multiple that we assign to the company.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ CR Mixc is a market leader in the shopping mall space, and bene ts from a strong shopping mall pipeline and rising retail sales. ▪ Residential property management of CR Mixc bene <sup>fi</sup>ts from a strong parent developer, with some projects from CR Group's other subsidiaries integrated, such as industrial parks and factories. ▪ We like CR Mixc for its commercial property management exposure, strong shopping mall operation capability and steady growth outlook.

![](images/5e1030de44be0f77fbec169d6baf3c85a2a5e04504a609aa2739e94208c8611c.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 25x 2025E P/E

HK\$49.78

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected GFA under management growth, driven by fasterthan-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increase, as property owners are willing to pay more for high service quality, which ultimately leads to faster-than-expected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry, as a consequence of more IPOs and rising government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 20x 2025E P/E

## HK\$39.82

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margins: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by steady support from CR Land, and on-track thirdparty GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant fee increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## 12x 2025E P/E

HK\$23.89

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected GFA under management growth, dragged down by slower delivery schedule from CR Land and weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

Need More Patience Before Turning Positive

## HK\$5.91PRICE TARGET

Base case, applying an 8x target P/E to our 2025 EPS estimate. Our target P/E multiple is derived from our industry scorecard, based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro le, parent developer, <sup>fi</sup>nancials and cash <sup>fl</sup>ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro <sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ The DVAS segment has been shrinking further amid weak property sales. ▪ More time is needed to regain growth momentum after the earnings reset. ▪ Dividend resumption timeline yet to be clear.

![](images/8ae8f92be98204bc91aed8abcbc97afb82600b9d1e7535c8d31fb68e03401496.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 15x 2025E P/E

HK\$11.08

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected GFA under management growth driven by fasterthan-expected third-party GFA expansion or M&A. Higher-than-expected property management fee increase as property owners attach higher value to high service quality, which ultimately leads to fasterthan-expected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the sector due to more IPOs and rising social and government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 8x 2025E P/E

## HK\$5.91

Steady GFA and VAS growth with relative stable margin: Steady growth in GFA under management, backed by on-track third-party GFA expansion and no substantial termination of existing projects. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term. Gross margin also to maintain a stable level. Community <sup>fi</sup>VAS recovery stay on track. Relative high impairment ratio (vs. 2021 level) to digest the A/R from parent developer.

## BEAR CASE

## 5x 2025E P/E

HK\$3.69

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected GFA under management growth, dragged down by slower delivery schedule from Jinke Property and weaker-than-expected thirdparty GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-thanexpected community VAS development further cap earnings upside. Lower-thanexpected margin level due to rising labor costs and higher-than-expected contribution from low-margin projects.

## RISKS TO PT/RATING

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Better-than-expected third-party GFA expansion or M&A in property service management

Better-than-expected growth in community VAS

Stronger-than-expected property market recovery

Strong policy signal to help resolve developers' liquidity issue

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Slower-than-expected GFA growth on intensifying competition

Slower-than-expected community VAS monetization

Parent nancials amid further credit tightening

## Risk Reward - Poly Property Servic<sub>Risk</sub> <sub>Reward</sub> <sub>–</sub> <sub>Poly</sub> <sub>Property</sub> <sub>Services</sub> <sub>Co</sub> <sub>Ltd</sub> <sub>(6049.HK)</sub>

Solid Growth with High Visibility

## HK\$44.07PRICE TARGET

We apply a 13x target P/E to our 2025 EPS estimate to arrive at our price target. Our target P/E multiple is derived from our industry scorecard, based on seven metrics: GFA growth pro<sup>fi</sup>le, parent developer, <sup>fi</sup>nancials and cash <sup>fl</sup>ows, revenue quality, service quality, pro<sup>fi</sup>tability, and incentive scheme. Poly Property Services has a diversi <sup>fi</sup>ed portfolio mix and rapid growth potential, in our view.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We still view the company’s fundamentals as solid, and its unrivaled edge in public
service as a strength in third-party expansion competition.
▪ Parent developer with SOE background with no liquidity issue.
▪ We see high earnings visibility in medium term, with c.10% earnings CAGR projected for the next few years.

![](images/1260506d9a44305391e0975b81080142af9d34c251e7c58ede119839fa24c0ab.jpg)

Risk Reward Themes Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 20x 2025E P/E

## HK\$67.80 BASE CASE

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected growth in GFA under management, driven by faster-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increases as property owners value high-quality service more, which ultimately leads to faster-thanexpected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry from more IPOs and rising social and government attention, driving valuation upward.

## 13x 2025E P/E

## HK\$44.07

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by steady support from Poly Developments, and ontrack third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## HK\$23.73

## 7x 2025E P/E

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than-expected growth in GFA under management, hampered by a slower delivery schedule from Poly Developments, along with weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

Slower-than-expected third-party GFA growth.

Failure to raise property management fees, resulting in margin squeeze due to rising labor costs.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Powerlong CM's commercial property management business model differentiates it from property management peers, with more stable margins. However, the recent challenging consumption environment has weighed on SSSG and rental growth. ▪ We see potential impairment risk from related-party accounts receivable. ▪ We expect dividend resumption for 2024's final payout, and could turn more constructive with a more clear dividend policy ahead.

![](images/16a77b72a7ec5ad9f4e9d5f44d8ff172ee96ba2b12dabd15f1af0cf117394f2e.jpg)

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## 6x 2025E P/E

HK\$5.43

Growth with better-than-expected margin: 1) Higher-than-expected GFA under management growth. 2) Higher-thanexpected property management fee increase, as property owners are willing to pay more for high service quality, which ultimately leads to faster-than-expected margin expansion. 3) Valuation re-rating due to its commercial property management edge. 4) Gross margin improves further.

## BASE CASE

## 3x 2025E P/E

## HK\$2.72

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by on-track support from Powerlong RE and third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## 2x 2025E P/E

HK\$1.81

Slower growth in GFA, low mall occupancy, slower community VAS: 1) Slower growth in GFA under management than in our base case, hampered by weaker third-party GFA expansion or slower mall opening schedule. 2) Occupancy rate of shopping malls remaining low. 3) Slower community VAS development that caps any further earnings upside potential.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Company has high-quality portfolio of GFA under management with improving third party expansion capability and large exposure to tier 1 and 2 cities. ▪ DVAS segment may further shrink amid weak property sales. ▪ Company needs time to regain growth momentum and prove organic growth ability after digesting parentco's liquidity risk.

## BULL CASE

## 12x 2025E P/E

HK\$2.62

Accelerating GFA growth with better-thanexpected margin: Higher-than-expected GFA under management growth, driven by fasterthan-expected third-party GFA expansion. Higher-than-expected property management fee increase, as property owners are willing to pay more for high service quality, which ultimately leads to faster-than-expected margin expansion. Increasing interest in the industry, as a consequence of more IPOs and rising government attention, driving valuation upward.

## BASE CASE

## 8x 2025E P/E

## HK\$1.75

Stable GFA growth with steady ASP and normalized margin: Stable growth in GFA under management, backed by on-track third-party GFA expansion. We expect relatively stable property management fees with no signi cant increases in the near term.

## BEAR CASE

## 3x 2025E P/E

HK\$0.66

Slower-than-expected GFA growth with margin squeeze: Slower-than expected GFA under management growth, dragged down by slower delivery schedule from Sunac and weaker-than-expected third-party GFA expansion. Tightening regulation on management fees and slower-than-expected VAS development further cap earnings upside.

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of al ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index
